# UseSafeWeb.com — Master Plan and Work Breakdown Structure

**Plan date:** 2026-08-27  
**Repository state rechecked through:** `Yaserbayad/UseSafeWeb.com` `main` at pre-amendment head `9b1498fa955cf2e9e4aa8d8a05f705413ae8c438`  
**Governance status:** **Owner-designated evolving centralized master plan**. This file is the authoritative planning/WBS baseline once this amendment is persisted and fetch-verified; `CURRENT_STATE.md` remains the authoritative live execution checkpoint for the current G-02 state.  
**Artifact persistence state:** Owner-authorised parent-dashboard/AdGuard-control-plane amendment persisted to GitHub `main` on 2026-08-27; exact resulting commit/blob is fetch-verified before completion is claimed.  
**Planning horizon:** Original inception through the end of Month 12 of real public operation.  
**Schedule model:** Historical / Current / Pre-Experiment / Experiment / Pre-Development / Development / Pilot / Pre-Launch / Launch / M1–M12 / Year-1 Close. Future calendar dates are intentionally not fabricated.  

> Authority is split deliberately by purpose: `Plans/UseSafeWeb_Master_Plan.md` governs the evolving program plan/WBS and approved future architecture; `CURRENT_STATE.md` governs the live execution checkpoint and current G-02 evidence. Monday.com/ClickUp remain derived execution views and cannot override this plan or `CURRENT_STATE.md`. Their pre-amendment transport assets are stale after this structural change and must be regenerated/reconciled before governed use.

## Output A — Executive Master Plan

### 1. Project objective

Deliver and operate **UseSafeWeb — First Phone Safety Setup**: a free, privacy-preserving, non-surveillance service that helps a parent or caregiver establish sensible safeguards around a roughly 10–12-year-old child’s first independently used smartphone. The validated service concept coordinates relevant native Apple/Google controls, real AdGuard-backed encrypted DNS baseline protection, one relevant external-service safeguard, and a truthful Protection Map. **Owner decision 2026-08-27:** the first coded MCP, if/when G-04 through G-07 authorise implementation, also includes a lightweight authenticated parent dashboard for device setup/management, protection state, curated controls, Protection Map, recovery and account/device lifecycle — without browsing/activity surveillance.

### 2. Current verified state

- Business evaluation phases **1–42 are complete**; the final decision remains **MODIFY — proceed to validation, not full launch**.
- **AdGuard** and DNS-over-HTTPS remain frozen technical decisions unless verified critical evidence requires reopening them.
- Experiment 1 is designed but **not authorised or started**. Minimal MCP build remains **not authorised until the existing validation/build gates pass**; however, its future scope is now owner-approved to include the lightweight authenticated parent dashboard and thin AdGuard control-plane integration defined in this amendment. Real MCP pilot, public launch, supporter payment, paid acquisition, and geographic expansion remain gate-controlled.
- Validation readiness **G-02 is in progress**. Current executable branches are the UK representative/Article-27 and ICO actions plus Azure West Europe/AdGuard deployment and verification; final notices and LIA/DPIA residual approval follow those branches.
- Pilot data path remains Azure `westeurope` (Netherlands) → AdGuard → Quad9 `https://dns10.quad9.net/dns-query`, ECS disabled, with no US node in Experiment 1.
- The corrected WBS distinguishes **Ready**, **Planned**, **Deferred**, and true **Blocked** work. Future gate-dependent work is no longer falsely classified as blocked.
- The amended WBS contains **786 non-root WBS records / 542 execution tasks**, **174 deliverables**, **60 phases**, and **10 lifecycle stages**.

### 3. Planning horizon

The plan reconstructs inception and feasibility, preserves completed evaluation work, governs current validation readiness, runs the bounded concierge experiment, conditionally defines/builds/tests a minimal MCP, conducts the real MCP pilot, prepares a UK launch only after evidence gates pass, operates through M12, and closes with the Year-1/Year-2 decision.

### 5. Major workstreams

Program governance and decision control; product/business strategy; customer and behavioral validation; product management and requirements; DNS/network engineering; Azure/cloud/DevOps; software/backend/frontend engineering; mobile onboarding/device compatibility; UX/UI/accessibility/content; security/privacy/data protection/safeguarding/legal; QA/release engineering; observability/SRE/recovery/incident management; support/customer success; branding/communications/marketing/growth/partnerships; finance/admin/procurement/vendor management; analytics/KPIs; evidence-triggered scaling; geographic-expansion readiness; and controlled pause/pivot/wind-down.

### 6. Critical execution network

A dated, resource-loaded CPM path does not yet exist and is not fabricated. `Critical_Path=Yes` therefore means **current G-02 gate-critical unfinished work only** and is recalculated when the active gate changes. The current network is:

1. Resolve UK representative/Article-27 path and ICO fee assessment in parallel with Azure West Europe provisioning/hardening.
2. Deploy/configure AdGuard, DNS/TLS endpoint, privacy-minimal logging/statistics/anonymisation/ECS/upstream settings, and verify the actual data path.
3. Reconcile final notices/contacts with deployed reality and approve final LIA/DPIA residual risk.
4. Assemble G-02 evidence, decide G-02, and update canonical current state.
5. Separately complete the synthetic rehearsal and other Experiment-1 operating pack items for **G-03**; G-02 no longer incorrectly depends on that rehearsal.

### 7. Major milestones

The milestone register contains **22 milestones**. M-020 remains an evidence-triggered early scale review; M-021 preserves the owner-approved 500-user formalisation trigger. M-004 now tracks owner-authorised centralized-plan persistence/verification rather than a deferred publication decision.

### 8. Governance gates

**18 gates (G-00 through G-17)** govern progression. G-00/G-01 are historical, G-02 is current, G-15 governs scale/formalisation, G-16 independently governs geographic expansion, and G-17 closes Year 1.

### 9. Main dependencies

- Legal/privacy authority and verified technical configuration are parallel prerequisites; neither substitutes for the other.
- G-02 does **not** depend on Experiment-1 rehearsal. G-03 depends on both G-02 closure and the synthetic rehearsal/operating pack.
- Software implementation depends on a positive Experiment-1 decision and G-05/G-06/G-07; real MCP pilot depends on G-08/G-09/G-10; production launch depends on G-11/G-12/G-13.
- 500 active users is an organisational/commercial review trigger, not a geographic-expansion prerequisite and not a legal threshold.
- Recurring/event relationships are represented as operating/trigger inputs, not false finish-to-start dependencies on tasks that never complete.

### 10. Major risks

The largest exposures remain unresolved legal/data readiness; weak behavioral activation or incremental value; DNS persistence/compatibility; support burden; privacy/logging leakage; DNS/service outage or abuse; stale platform guidance; misleading safety claims; weak supporter/channel economics; key-person dependency; and premature scaling/expansion.

### 11. Year-1 operating model

Year-1 recurring activities are **Planned until public operation actually starts**; their cadence remains in the `Recurrence` field. Once the operating stage is activated, the relevant items transition to Recurring. This prevents future routines from appearing already active today.

### 12. Key growth and scaling triggers

- **Evidence-triggered scale review:** only when observed capacity, support, reliability, cost, abuse, or growth data warrants a decision.
- **500 verified active users:** organisational/commercial formalisation review; earlier material revenue/contracts/staffing/tax/legal/partner/risk triggers can activate that review sooner.
- Geographic expansion is independent of 500 users and requires **G-16**, a specific next-market assessment, stable UK evidence, and owner approval.
- No automatic US deployment is planned.

### 13. Current next-stage recommendation

**Remain at Stage 2 / G-02.** The next executable work is the legal branch (UK representation route + ICO self-assessment), technical branch (Azure topology/provisioning then AdGuard/DNS/TLS verification), and parallel ready preparation tasks whose hard dependencies are satisfied. The owner has now designated this GitHub plan as the evolving centralized planning baseline. This does **not** change the current execution sequence: remain at Stage 2 / G-02 until its evidence gates pass.

### 14. Owner-approved first-coded MCP parent dashboard and AdGuard control-plane amendment — 2026-08-27

**Decision and sequencing.** The owner has explicitly approved a simple, lightweight, direct, polished parent dashboard as part of the **first coded MCP**. This supersedes the earlier blanket assumption that the coded product must remain accountless or dashboard-free. It does **not** authorise software implementation before the existing behavioral-validation and development gates. Experiment 1 remains the bounded concierge validation; Stage 4 defines/freezes the amended product; Stage 5 implements it only after G-07 PASS.

**Selected planning architecture.** The minimum architecture is:

`UseSafeWeb public site → Firebase Authentication (Google provider) → secure server-managed UseSafeWeb session → minimal parent/device datastore → allowlisted server-side AdGuard Home adapter → persistent high-entropy ClientID per device → direct DoH data plane → AdGuard Home → Quad9 dns10`.

The **control plane** is the authenticated UseSafeWeb application/API and minimal account/device metadata. The **DNS data plane** remains direct device-to-AdGuard encrypted DNS; DNS traffic is not proxied through the dashboard application. The browser never receives AdGuard administrator credentials and can never submit arbitrary `/control` requests.

**Authentication decision.** Initial authentication is **Firebase Authentication using Google sign-in on the base Spark/no-cost product, without upgrading to Identity Platform and without SMS/phone authentication**. The server validates Firebase identity and exchanges it for a secure `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite` session cookie with CSRF protection and revocation handling. This is selected for minimum development/operations burden and zero expected incremental authentication-service cost at the project's initial scale under the current pricing model. Pricing, quotas, terms, processing/transfer posture and exit triggers must be reverified at G-06/G-12 and during Year 1; no provider is described as “free forever.”

**AdGuard Home feasibility/cost/licensing decision.** Current official AdGuard Home sources confirm the self-hosted project is GPL-3.0 and its authenticated REST-like administration API is part of AdGuard Home under `/control`; the current client API supports add/update/delete/search, persistent ClientIDs, per-client filtering-related controls, upstreams, `ignore_querylog` and `ignore_statistics`. No separate AdGuard Home API/subscription fee is evidenced for this self-hosted integration; Azure/infrastructure remains separately chargeable. UseSafeWeb must deploy AdGuard as a separate server process and integrate through its API rather than embed/fork GPL code. Specialist licence review is triggered before distributing modified AdGuard binaries, combining/linking GPL code into UseSafeWeb, or making a materially different licensing use.

**Critical privacy invariant.** Every managed UseSafeWeb AdGuard client must explicitly set and regression-test `ignore_querylog=true` and `ignore_statistics=true` (or the current equivalent verified against the deployed AdGuard version); defaults must never be relied on. Dashboard value comes from configuration, setup/protection state, Protection Map, curated controls and recovery — **not** DNS query history, top domains, browsing timelines or child behavioral analytics.

**Minimum parent dashboard value set.** Google sign-in/sign-out/account deletion; device list/cards and parent-facing nicknames without requiring the child's legal name; add/configure/verify/reinstall/replace/revoke/remove device; truthful protection/setup state; per-device Protection Map; a deliberately small evidence-backed set of parent-understandable AdGuard-backed controls; self-service help, false-positive/recovery and privacy routes. Any control that cannot be explained, verified, supported and kept privacy-safe is excluded from the first version.

**Explicit non-goals retained.** No child account; browsing/query/top-domain history; activity timeline; location/message/contact/photo/social-content monitoring; broad DNS administration console; unrestricted AdGuard policy editor; comprehensive screen-time replacement; GROW automation; native app; school portal; or safety feature paywall without later explicit evidence/gates.

**Minimal data boundary.** Store only parent authentication identity mapping, internal parent ID, internal device ID, opaque AdGuard client identifier/ClientID, optional parent-facing nickname, necessary platform/configuration/Protection-Map state and strictly necessary operational timestamps. Parent/account metadata is separated from child browsing activity; no DNS/domain-history store is introduced.

**Primary official sources verified 2026-08-27.** AdGuard Home repository/license/OpenAPI/client documentation: `https://github.com/AdguardTeam/AdGuardHome`, `https://github.com/AdguardTeam/AdGuardHome/blob/master/LICENSE.txt`, `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/AdguardTeam/AdGuardHome/master/openapi/openapi.yaml`, `https://github.com/AdguardTeam/AdGuardHome/wiki/Clients`. Firebase/Google authentication, session and pricing documentation: `https://firebase.google.com/docs/auth/web/google-signin`, `https://firebase.google.com/docs/auth/admin/manage-cookies`, `https://firebase.google.com/docs/auth/admin/verify-id-tokens`, `https://firebase.google.com/pricing`, `https://cloud.google.com/identity-platform/pricing`, `https://developers.google.com/identity/protocols/oauth2/policies`.

> This is a **planning/architecture decision**, not product implementation authorisation. Any time-sensitive API, pricing, quota, licence, transfer or vendor fact must be reverified at the implementation/release gate that depends on it.

## Execution Governance Semantics

### Status definitions

- **Completed:** acceptance criteria proven with sufficient evidence.
- **Completed — evidence incomplete:** work is authoritatively recorded as complete, but direct execution evidence is incomplete; it does not automatically satisfy an evidence-gated successor that requires direct verification.
- **In Progress:** execution has actually begun.
- **Ready:** all hard predecessors and current authorization conditions are satisfied; the task can start now.
- **Planned:** valid future work whose hard prerequisites/gate are not yet satisfied; this is **not** a blocker.
- **Blocked:** a task that otherwise should be executable now but is prevented by a specific unresolved impediment. No task is currently classified Blocked after this correction; current G-02 blockers are represented as outstanding prerequisites.
- **Deferred:** intentionally postponed until a named trigger or owner decision.
- **Recurring:** an operating/governance cadence that is active now. Future Year-1 cadences remain Planned until operation starts.
- **Not Applicable:** explicitly excluded under current approved scope.
- **Cancelled/Superseded:** historical work that no longer governs.

### Parent roll-up rules

1. A parent cannot be Completed while a mandatory child is Ready, Planned, In Progress, or Blocked.
2. Optional or trigger-based children must be explicitly Deferred, Not Applicable, or Cancelled/Superseded before a parent can close.
3. Any active Recurring/In-Progress child makes the current parent In Progress.
4. Historical parents may be `Completed — evidence incomplete` where the authoritative completion assertion exists but direct evidence remains incomplete.
5. Parent roll-ups are recalculated whenever a child status materially changes.

### Deterministic next-executable-task rule

1. Read the latest explicit owner decision and canonical `CURRENT_STATE.md`/gate state.
2. Apply any owner decision that supersedes older repository state and flag the repository synchronization need.
3. Select Level-4/5 tasks with `Status=Ready` in the currently authorised stage; do not select Planned/Deferred work.
4. Confirm every hard `Dependency_ID` is satisfied and the current gate permits the work. `Completed — evidence incomplete` is insufficient where the successor requires direct verification.
5. Order eligible work: current safety/legal/security blocker → current gate-critical work → dependency-chain constraint → High priority → supporting parallel work.
6. Execute one bounded task/coherent serial unit, verify acceptance evidence, then update task status/evidence.
7. Recalculate parent roll-ups, newly Ready tasks, current gate-critical network, risks/decisions, and current checkpoint.
8. Persist to GitHub only when authorized; fetch back and verify every material canonical write.

## Output G — CSV and Import Mapping

**Current artifact state (verified 2026-08-27):** the centralized GitHub `Plans/` directory contains this Markdown plan but does **not** contain a current standalone `UseSafeWeb_Master_Plan_WBS.csv`. The repository's existing ClickUp transport chunks are pre-amendment derived assets and must not be presented as the current 38-column source. This amendment therefore does **not fabricate missing 38-column fields**.

The embedded WBS below is the current structural planning model. Before Monday.com/ClickUp or another tracker is used for governed execution, `USW-02.01.02.005` must regenerate the complete platform-neutral CSV from a current full-field source and verify it against this plan revision.

### Required future 38-column CSV contract

The regenerated CSV must preserve stable `WBS_ID`/`Parent_WBS_ID`, level/type/stage/phase/workstream, task name/description, status, owner/support roles, periods, hard `Dependency_IDs`, gate/milestone, priority/risk, acceptance/verification/evidence, recurrence/trigger, source reference, notes and current critical-path signal without inventing unavailable values. Hard dependencies remain finish-to-start predecessors only; recurring/event relationships remain trigger/notes semantics.

### Recommended Monday.com mapping

- Item Name → `Task_Name`; Group → `Stage` or `Phase`; Status → `Status`; People/Owner → `Owner_Role`; Priority → `Priority`; Timeline → `Start_Period`/`Due_Period`; Dependency → `Dependency_IDs`; Tags → `Workstream`/`Department_Function`; Milestone → `Milestone_ID`; Notes → `Task_Description`/`Acceptance_Criteria`/`Notes`; custom fields → `WBS_ID`, `Parent_WBS_ID`, `Level`, `Gate_ID`, `Risk_Level`, `Recurrence`, `Trigger_Condition`, `Source_Reference`, `Critical_Path`.

### Recommended ClickUp mapping

- Task Name → `Task_Name`; Parent → match `Parent_WBS_ID` to `WBS_ID`; Status → `Status`; Assignee/Role → `Owner_Role`; Priority → `Priority`; Start/Due → `Start_Period`/`Due_Period`; Dependencies → `Dependency_IDs`; Space → UseSafeWeb.com; Folder → `Stage`; List → `Phase`; Tags → `Workstream`/`Department_Function`/`Gate_ID`; Milestone → `Milestone_ID`; remaining governance fields → custom fields.

## Final Consistency Pass

- The 2026-08-27 owner decision is represented as a future **first-coded-MCP** scope change, not as permission to skip Experiment 1 or G-04–G-07.
- Every explicit “accountless/no-dashboard” assumption found in the current plan was reconciled: Experiment-1 accountless/concierge behavior is preserved historically; Stage 4–8 coded-product wording now reflects authenticated parent management.
- The dashboard is deliberately a **management/control surface**, not an activity/surveillance surface; raw DNS administration, query history, top domains and child behavioral monitoring remain non-goals.
- Firebase/Google authentication and AdGuard Home/API/licensing/cost facts are dated planning evidence and have mandatory re-verification triggers before architecture/release approval.
- The browser never receives AdGuard admin credentials; ClientID is never application authorization; parent/device ownership is enforced server-side; the adapter is allowlisted rather than a generic proxy.
- Explicit per-client no-querylog/no-statistics controls are requirements/tests, not assumed defaults.
- Current Stage-2/G-02 work, 500-user formalisation logic and geographic-expansion gates remain unchanged.
- WBS hierarchy/dependencies are acyclic and all new implementation tasks are Planned behind existing gates.
- The centralized Markdown plan no longer falsely claims a current standalone 38-column CSV; tracker synchronization remains a controlled derived-artifact task.

## Controlled Maintenance Rule

Do not fork competing master plans. `Plans/UseSafeWeb_Master_Plan.md` is the evolving centralized planning/WBS baseline; `CURRENT_STATE.md` remains the live execution checkpoint. Update both when an approved decision materially changes future scope or current execution authority. Regenerate the complete full-field WBS CSV and derived Monday/ClickUp views after material WBS changes before relying on them for governed execution. Re-run hierarchy/dependency/cycle/status/gate/risk/decision/privacy/source audits after each structural amendment, and reverify time-sensitive vendor/API/pricing/licensing facts at the gate that depends on them.
